    - Other rare causes (e.g., glucocorticoid-remediable hyperaldosteronism, adrenal carcinoma).
- **Diagnosis:** Screening involves measuring the serum aldosterone concentration (ARR) and plasma renin activity (PRA) to calculate the ARR/PRA ratio. Confirmatory tests include saline infusion test, oral sodium loading test, or fludrocortisone suppression test.
- **Lateralization:** Once primary hyperaldosteronism is confirmed, determining if it's unilateral (APA) or bilateral (IHA) is crucial because unilateral APA is potentially curable with unilateral adrenalectomy, while bilateral IHA requires medical management (mineralocorticoid receptor antagonists like spironolactone or eplerenone).
- **Imaging Limitations:** Adrenal CT can identify adrenal masses but often cannot reliably distinguish APA from IHA or identify non-functioning incidentalomas. Sensitivity for APA is around 70-80%.
- **Adrenal Vein Sampling (AVS):** The gold standard for lateralization. It involves cannulating both adrenal veins and the inferior vena cava (IVC) and measuring hormone levels (aldosterone and cortisol).
    - **Selective Cannulation:** Confirmed by comparing adrenal vein cortisol levels to IVC cortisol levels. A ratio > 5:1 (with ACTH stimulation) is generally required.
    - **Lateralization Index:** Calculated as the ratio of aldosterone/cortisol (or aldosterone/19-norcorticosterone) on the side with the higher aldosterone level divided by the ratio on the contralateral side.
    - **Interpretation:** A lateralization ratio ≥ 4:1 indicates unilateral aldosterone production (APA), suggesting potential benefit from unilateral adrenalectomy. A ratio < 2:1 indicates bilateral aldosterone production (IHA), favoring medical management. Ratios between 2:1 and 4:1 are indeterminate.
- **Cosyntropin Infusion:** Used during AVS to stimulate cortisol production, making selective cannulation easier and more reliable, especially on the right side which drains directly into the IVC.

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. The patient presents with classic signs and symptoms of primary hyperaldosteronism (resistant hypertension, hypokalemia).
2. Laboratory tests confirm primary hyperaldosteronism (high aldosterone, low renin).
3. Adrenal CT is inconclusive for lateralization.
4. The question asks for the gold standard procedure to differentiate unilateral APA from bilateral IHA before considering surgery.
5. AVS is the established gold standard procedure for this purpose, providing direct measurement of aldosterone secretion from each adrenal gland.
6. The AVS results described in the vignette (higher aldosterone/cortisol ratio on the left) support unilateral disease, consistent with the presence of a left adrenal adenoma.
7. Therefore, AVS is the correct answer.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) Adrenal CT scan with contrast enhancement:** While useful for initial evaluation and identifying adrenal masses, CT has limited sensitivity and specificity for distinguishing APA from IHA or identifying non-functioning incidentalomas. It is not the gold standard for lateralization.
- **(B) Scintigraphy with 123I-6-iodomethylnorcholesterol (NP-59):** This is an older imaging technique (adrenal scintigraphy) that can sometimes help lateralize aldosterone production, but it has lower accuracy than AVS, especially in patients with bilateral disease or small unilateral adenomas. It is not considered the gold standard.
- **(D) Measurement of serum 18-hydroxycorticosterone:** 18-hydroxycorticosterone is a mineralocorticoid precursor that can be elevated in bilateral adrenal hyperplasia but is not a standard diagnostic or lateralization test for primary hyperaldosteronism.
- **(E) Measurement of serum deoxycorticosterone (DOC):** DOC is a potent mineralocorticoid produced by the adrenal cortex. Elevated DOC levels can cause mineralocorticoid excess, but it is not the primary hormone measured in primary hyperaldosteronism, and its measurement is not used for lateralization in this context. DOC levels might be elevated in certain rare forms of hyperaldosteronism or adrenal carcinoma, but not typically in APA or IHA.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)

| Feature                     | Unilateral APA                                  | Bilateral IHA                                  |
| :-------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- |
| **Etiology**                | Single aldosterone-producing adenoma             | Hyperplasia of both adrenal glands               |
| **Prevalence**              | ~30-40%                                         | ~60-70%                                         |
| **Lateralization**          | Unilateral                                      | Bilateral                                       |
| **Treatment**               | Unilateral adrenalectomy (potentially curative) | Medical therapy (MRA)                            |
| **Gold Standard Diagnosis** | AVS (Lateralization Ratio ≥ 4:1)                | AVS (Lateralization Ratio < 2:1)                |
| **CT Findings**             | Often a unilateral nodule                       | Often bilateral nodularity or normal appearance |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
Bilateral adrenal vein sampling (AVS) is the gold standard procedure for differentiating unilateral aldosterone-producing adenoma (APA) from bilateral idiopathic hyperaldosteronism (IHA) in patients with confirmed primary hyperaldosteronism, especially when adrenal imaging is inconclusive. This distinction is critical because unilateral APA is potentially curable with surgery, while bilateral IHA requires lifelong medical management. The lateralization ratio (aldosterone/cortisol ratio on the dominant side divided by the ratio on the non-dominant side) obtained during AVS guides this decision, with a ratio ≥ 4:1 indicating unilateral disease and < 2:1 indicating bilateral disease.

---

### Question 40: Conn Syndrome Pathophysiology
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The molecular mechanism underlying autonomous aldosterone production in a subset of aldosterone-producing adenomas (APAs), specifically the role of somatic mutations in the KCNJ5 gene encoding the Kir3.4 potassium channel, leading to membrane depolarization and increased CYP11B2 transcription.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old woman with a history of hypertension, resistant to multiple antihypertensive medications, is evaluated for secondary causes. Physical examination reveals mild central obesity and no specific signs of Cushing syndrome or virilization. Laboratory results show serum sodium of 148 mEq/L (normal: 135-145), serum potassium of 3.1 mEq/L (normal: 3.5-5.0), plasma renin activity (PRA) of <5 ng/mL/hr (normal: 1.5-25), and serum aldosterone of 35 ng/dL (normal: 4-21). An abdominal CT scan reveals a 1.4 cm, lipid-rich nodule in the left adrenal gland. Genetic sequencing of DNA extracted from the resected adrenal adenoma tissue identifies a heterozygous somatic missense mutation (p.G151R) in the KCNJ5 gene. Which of the following ion channels is directly implicated in the pathophysiology of aldosterone hypersecretion in this patient's adenoma due to this specific mutation?

(A) Voltage-gated sodium channel (SCN5A)
(B) Voltage-gated calcium channel (CACNA1H)
(C) Inward rectifying potassium channel (KCNJ5 / Kir3.4)
(D) Epithelial sodium channel (ENaC)
(E) Large-conductance calcium-activated potassium channel (BK channel / KCNG1)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 58-year-old woman with resistant hypertension. This raises suspicion for secondary hypertension, including primary aldosteronism (Conn syndrome).
- **Laboratory Findings:** Hypernatremia (148 mEq/L), hypokalemia (3.1 mEq/L), suppressed PRA (<5 ng/mL/hr), and elevated aldosterone (35 ng/dL). This classic biochemical profile strongly suggests primary aldosteronism, characterized by autonomous aldosterone production leading to sodium retention, potassium excretion, volume expansion, and subsequent suppression of renin.
- **Imaging:** CT scan shows a left adrenal nodule (1.4 cm), consistent with an aldosterone-producing adenoma (APA).
- **Genetic Finding:** Somatic missense mutation (p.G151R) in the KCNJ5 gene. This is the crucial piece of information linking the clinical presentation to a specific molecular mechanism. KCNJ5 encodes the Kir3.4 potassium channel.
- **Question Stem:** Asks which ion channel is directly implicated in the pathophysiology of aldosterone hypersecretion due to the KCNJ5 mutation.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Molecular Biophysics (肾上腺皮质生理机制与分子生物学推导)
- **Adrenal Steroidogenesis:** Aldosterone synthesis occurs in the zona glomerulosa of the adrenal cortex, regulated primarily by the renin-angiotensin system (Ang II) and plasma potassium levels. The final step involves the conversion of 11-deoxycorticosterone to corticosterone by 11β-hydroxylase (CYP11B1) or the conversion of corticosterone to aldosterone by aldosterone synthase (CYP11B2).
- **Regulation of CYP11B2:** In normal adrenal cells, CYP11B2 expression is tightly regulated. In APAs, autonomous production occurs due to dysregulation of this enzyme.
- **Role of KCNJ5 (Kir3.4):** KCNJ5 encodes the G-protein-gated inward rectifying potassium channel Kir3.4. These channels are expressed in adrenal cortical cells, particularly in the zona glomerulosa. They play a critical role in maintaining the resting membrane potential.
- **Mechanism of KCNJ5 Mutations:** Somatic gain-of-function mutations in KCNJ5 (found in ~40-50% of APAs) alter the channel's function. Specifically, mutations often affect the selectivity filter (e.g., the G151R mutation mentioned), impairing the channel's ability to selectively conduct potassium ions ($K^+$). Instead, the mutated channel allows significant inward leak of sodium ions ($Na^+$) into the cell.
- **Consequences of Na+ Leak:** The influx of positive charge ($Na^+$) causes depolarization of the cell membrane.
- **Depolarization and CaMK Activation:** Membrane depolarization opens voltage-gated calcium channels, leading to increased influx of extracellular calcium ions ($Ca^{2+}$). This sustained elevation in intracellular calcium activates calcium/calmodulin-dependent protein kinase II (CaMKII).
- **CYP11B2 Transcription:** Activated CaMKII phosphorylates transcription factors, such as CREB (cAMP response element-binding protein) and potentially others, which then bind to the promoter region of the CYP11B2 gene, driving its transcription and leading to increased aldosterone synthase enzyme levels.
- **Autonomous Aldosterone Production:** The increased CYP11B2 enzyme levels result in constitutive, autonomous production of aldosterone, independent of normal regulatory signals (Ang II, K+).
- **Other Mutations:** Somatic mutations in ATP1A1 (encoding the catalytic subunit of the plasma membrane Na+/K+-ATPase) are found in another significant subset (~30-40%) of APAs. These mutations lead to constitutive activation of the Na+/K+-ATPase, pumping more Na+ into the cell and K+ out, also causing membrane depolarization and subsequent activation of CaMKII and CYP11B2 transcription.

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. The patient presents with clinical and biochemical features of primary aldosteronism (hypertension, hypokalemia, hypernatremia, suppressed renin, elevated aldosterone).
2. Imaging confirms an adrenal adenoma, the most common cause of primary aldosteronism.
3. Genetic analysis reveals a somatic mutation in the KCNJ5 gene within the adenoma.
4. The question asks for the ion channel directly involved in the pathophysiology caused by this mutation.
5. KCNJ5 encodes the Kir3.4 potassium channel.
6. Mutations in KCNJ5 lead to loss of potassium selectivity and increased sodium influx.
7. Sodium influx causes membrane depolarization.
8. Depolarization activates calcium influx and CaMKII.
9. CaMKII drives transcription of CYP11B2, leading to autonomous aldosterone production.
10. Therefore, the KCNJ5 potassium channel is the direct molecular culprit implicated in this specific mechanism of Conn syndrome.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) Voltage-gated sodium channel (SCN5A):** While voltage-gated sodium channels are crucial for neuronal and muscle excitability, they are not the primary ion channel directly mutated or functionally altered in the common molecular mechanisms driving autonomous aldosterone production in APAs. Mutations in SCN5A are associated with cardiac arrhythmias (e.g., Long QT syndrome, Brugada syndrome). Incorrect.
- **(B) Voltage-gated calcium channel (CACNA1H):** Voltage-gated calcium channels are involved in the depolarization-induced calcium influx step downstream of the initial membrane depolarization caused by KCNJ5 or ATP1A1 mutations. However, they are not the primary channel directly affected by the KCNJ5 mutation itself. Mutations in CACNA1H are associated with Timothy syndrome (Long QT, syndactyly, autism). Incorrect.
- **(C) Inward rectifying potassium channel (KCNJ5 / Kir3.4):** This is the correct answer. The vignette explicitly states a mutation in KCNJ5. As detailed above, mutations in this gene encoding the Kir3.4 channel disrupt its function, leading to sodium leak, depolarization, calcium influx, CaMKII activation, and increased CYP11B2 transcription, ultimately causing autonomous aldosterone hypersecretion. Correct.
- **(D) Epithelial sodium channel (ENaC):** ENaC is located on the apical membrane of principal cells in the collecting duct and is the target of aldosterone action, mediating sodium reabsorption and potassium secretion. While crucial for the *effects* of aldosterone, ENaC itself is not the channel directly mutated in the common molecular mechanisms causing autonomous *production* of aldosterone in APAs. Mutations in ENaC are associated with Liddle syndrome (gain-of-function) or pseudohypoaldosteronism type 1 (loss-of-function). Incorrect.
- **(E) Large-conductance calcium-activated potassium channel (BK channel / KCNG1):** BK channels are involved in repolarization in various excitable cells. They are not the primary channel directly implicated in the molecular pathogenesis of aldosterone-producing adenomas driven by KCNJ5 or ATP1A1 mutations. Incorrect.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)
| Molecular Defect in APA | Gene Involved | Protein Affected | Mechanism | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KCNJ5 Mutation | KCNJ5 | Kir3.4 (K+ channel) | Loss of K+ selectivity -> Na+ leak | Membrane depolarization -> Ca2+ influx -> CaMKII activation -> CYP11B2 upregulation |
| ATP1A1 Mutation | ATP1A1 | Na+/K+-ATPase α-subunit | Constitutive activation | Increased intracellular Na+ -> Membrane depolarization -> Ca2+ influx -> CaMKII activation -> CYP11B2 upregulation |
| CYP11B2 Somatic Mosaicism | CYP11B2 | Aldosterone Synthase | Increased enzyme expression in a subset of cells | Autonomous aldosterone production |
| cAMP-independent ACTH receptor activation | *Not typically a somatic mutation* | ACTH receptor | Constitutive activation | Increased cAMP -> PKA activation -> CYP11B2 upregulation |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
Somatic mutations in KCNJ5 (encoding the Kir3.4 potassium channel) are a frequent cause of aldosterone-producing adenomas. These mutations disrupt potassium selectivity, leading to sodium influx, membrane depolarization, calcium influx, CaMKII activation, and ultimately, autonomous upregulation of CYP11B2, the enzyme responsible for aldosterone synthesis. Understanding this molecular mechanism is crucial for comprehending the pathophysiology of a significant subset of Conn syndrome cases.

---

### Question 41: Glucocorticoid-Remediable Aldosteronism (GRA): Chimeric CYP11B1/CYP11B2 Gene Duplication
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Glucocorticoid-Remediable Aldosteronism (GRA) is an autosomal dominant disorder caused by a chimeric gene resulting from unequal crossing-over between the CYP11B1 and CYP11B2 genes, leading to ACTH-dependent aldosterone production in the zona fasciculata, which is suppressed by glucocorticoids. (GRA 是一种常染色体显性遗传病，由 CYP11B1 和 CYP11B2 基因之间不等交换引起的嵌合基因导致，从而使肾上腺皮质束区产生 ACTH 依赖性醛固酮，并通过糖皮质激素进行抑制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 14-year-old boy presents to his pediatrician complaining of frequent headaches and dizziness. His father and paternal grandfather both had severe hypertension diagnosed in their teens and suffered fatal hemorrhagic strokes in their early 30s. On examination, the patient's blood pressure is markedly elevated at 160/100 mmHg. Laboratory investigations reveal serum potassium of 2.8 mEq/L (normal: 3.5-5.0 mEq/L), serum sodium of 148 mEq/L (normal: 135-145 mEq/L), plasma renin activity (PRA) of <5 ng/mL/hr (normal: 8-25 ng/mL/hr), and serum aldosterone of 45 ng/dL (normal: 3-20 ng/dL). A low-dose dexamethasone suppression test (0.5 mg daily for 48 hours) is performed. Following dexamethasone administration, his blood pressure normalizes to 120/80 mmHg, serum potassium increases to 4.2 mEq/L, and serum aldosterone becomes undetectable. What genetic aberration produces glucocorticoid-remediable aldosteronism, rendering aldosterone synthase expression responsive exclusively to ACTH?

(A) A point mutation in the mineralocorticoid receptor gene leading to constitutive activation by cortisol.
(B) A deletion in the CYP11B1 gene resulting in decreased cortisol production and compensatory ACTH stimulation of the chimeric aldosterone synthase.
(C) Unequal crossing-over creating a chimeric CYP11B1 promoter / CYP11B2 coding sequence gene.
(D) A mutation in the angiotensin II receptor type 1 gene leading to constitutive activation and increased aldosterone synthesis.
(E) A duplication of the CYP11B2 gene leading to increased aldosterone synthase expression, which is normally regulated by angiotensin II.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 14-year-old boy with severe hypertension (160/100 mmHg), hypokalemia (2.8 mEq/L), hypernatremia (148 mEq/L), suppressed renin (<5 ng/mL/hr), and elevated aldosterone (45 ng/dL). This constellation points towards primary hyperaldosteronism (Conn syndrome).
- **Family History:** Father and paternal grandfather with early-onset severe hypertension and fatal hemorrhagic strokes. This suggests a genetic, autosomal dominant inheritance pattern, often associated with intracranial aneurysms in GRA.
- **Dexamethasone Suppression Test:** Low-dose dexamethasone (0.5 mg daily) normalizes blood pressure, corrects hypokalemia, and suppresses aldosterone to undetectable levels within 48 hours. This is the hallmark diagnostic feature of Glucocorticoid-Remediable Aldosteronism (GRA). The suppression indicates that the aldosterone production is driven by ACTH, which is inhibited by dexamethasone.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific genetic aberration responsible for GRA, highlighting the ACTH-dependent nature of aldosterone synthesis in this condition.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Molecular Biophysics (肾上腺皮质生理机制与分子生物学推导)
- **Normal Steroidogenesis:** Aldosterone synthesis occurs in the zona glomerulosa, primarily regulated by the renin-angiotensin-aldosterone system (RAAS) and plasma potassium levels. Cortisol synthesis occurs in the zona fasciculata, primarily regulated by ACTH from the pituitary. The genes CYP11B1 (encoding 11β-hydroxylase, required for cortisol synthesis) and CYP11B2 (encoding aldosterone synthase, the final enzyme in aldosterone synthesis) are located adjacent to each other on chromosome 8q24.
- **Glucocorticoid-Remediable Aldosteronism (GRA):** This is a rare autosomal dominant form of early-onset hypertension. It arises from an unequal crossing-over event during meiosis between the CYP11B1 and CYP11B2 genes. This creates a hybrid or chimeric gene.
- **Chimeric Gene Structure:** The chimeric gene contains the ACTH-responsive promoter region from the CYP11B1 gene fused to the coding sequence of the CYP11B2 gene (aldosterone synthase).
- **Ectopic Expression:** This chimeric gene is expressed primarily in the zona fasciculata (where CYP11B1 is normally expressed) and, to a lesser extent, in the zona glomerulosa.
- **ACTH-Dependent Aldosterone Synthesis:** Because the chimeric gene has the CYP11B1 promoter, the expression of aldosterone synthase in the fasciculata becomes dependent on ACTH stimulation. Normal diurnal ACTH rhythms lead to excessive, unregulated aldosterone production.
- **Suppression by Glucocorticoids:** Exogenous glucocorticoids (like dexamethasone) suppress pituitary ACTH secretion via negative feedback. This shuts down the ACTH-driven expression of the chimeric aldosterone synthase, leading to a rapid decrease in aldosterone synthesis, normalization of blood pressure, and correction of hypokalemia.
- **Clinical Consequences:** Early-onset severe hypertension, hypokalemia, suppressed renin, increased risk of intracranial aneurysms and hemorrhagic stroke.

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. The patient presents with hypertension, hypokalemia, suppressed renin, and elevated aldosterone, consistent with primary hyperaldosteronism.
2. The strong family history of early-onset hypertension and fatal strokes suggests a genetic cause.
3. The dramatic suppression of aldosterone and normalization of blood pressure/potassium with low-dose dexamethasone is pathognomonic for GRA.
4. GRA is caused by a genetic rearrangement between the CYP11B1 and CYP11B2 genes.
5. This rearrangement creates a chimeric gene where the ACTH-responsive promoter of CYP11B1 drives the expression of the aldosterone synthase (CYP11B2) coding sequence.
6. Therefore, aldosterone synthesis becomes dependent on ACTH and is suppressed by glucocorticoids.
7. Option (C) accurately describes this genetic aberration.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) A point mutation in the mineralocorticoid receptor gene leading to constitutive activation by cortisol.** This describes Apparent Mineralocorticoid Excess (AME) Type I, caused by mutations in the 11β-hydroxysteroid dehydrogenase type 2 (11β-HSD2) gene, leading to inability to convert cortisol to cortisone in mineralocorticoid target tissues (like the kidney), allowing cortisol to bind and activate the mineralocorticoid receptor. This causes hypertension, hypokalemia, and suppressed renin, but it is *not* suppressed by dexamethasone (which would increase cortisol levels further activating the MR).
- **(B) A deletion in the CYP11B1 gene resulting in decreased cortisol production and compensatory ACTH stimulation of the chimeric aldosterone synthase.** A deletion in CYP11B1 would cause 11β-hydroxylase deficiency (a form of CAH), leading to decreased cortisol and aldosterone, increased deoxycorticosterone (DOC), hypertension, hypokalemia, and virilization. While ACTH would be high, the aldosterone synthase itself would not be chimeric or ACTH-dependent in the way described for GRA. Furthermore, the vignette describes *elevated* aldosterone, not decreased.
- **(D) A mutation in the angiotensin II receptor type 1 gene leading to constitutive activation and increased aldosterone synthesis.** This is not a known cause of hyperaldosteronism. Mutations in the angiotensin II receptor type 1 gene are associated with hypertension, but not typically with primary hyperaldosteronism or dexamethasone suppression.
- **(E) A duplication of the CYP11B2 gene leading to increased aldosterone synthase expression, which is normally regulated by angiotensin II.** Gene duplication of CYP11B2 can cause familial hyperaldosteronism type II (FH-II), characterized by increased aldosterone production regulated normally by angiotensin II and potassium. Patients have hypertension, hypokalemia, suppressed renin, and elevated aldosterone, but aldosterone secretion is *not* suppressed by dexamethasone because the regulation is RAAS-dependent, not ACTH-dependent.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)

| Condition                     | Genetic Defect                                  | Aldosterone Regulation | Dexamethasone Effect | Key Features                                                                 |
| :---------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- | :--------------------- | :------------------ | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GRA                           | CYP11B1/CYP11B2 chimeric gene                   | ACTH-dependent         | Suppressed          | Early-onset HTN, hypokalemia, intracranial aneurysms, family history of stroke |
| AME Type I                    | 11β-HSD2 deficiency                              | MR activation by cortisol | No effect           | HTN, hypokalemia, suppressed renin                                            |
| 11β-Hydroxylase Deficiency (CAH) | CYP11B1 deletion/mutation                       | ACTH-dependent         | Variable            | HTN, hypokalemia, virilization, elevated DOC                                 |
| FH-II                         | CYP11B2 duplication/mutation                    | Ang II/K+-dependent    | No effect           | HTN, hypokalemia, suppressed renin, elevated aldosterone                     |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
Glucocorticoid-Remediable Aldosteronism (GRA) is a rare genetic disorder caused by a chimeric gene formed from unequal crossing-over between CYP11B1 and CYP11B2. This results in ACTH-dependent aldosterone synthesis in the zona fasciculata, which is uniquely suppressed by glucocorticoids like dexamethasone, differentiating it from other forms of primary hyperaldosteronism. The condition is associated with early-onset hypertension and increased risk of intracranial hemorrhage.
